0

\lesssim 1

Table 5.3
:
Values of the Yukawa alignment parameters
\zeta_{f}
in the Type-I,II,X,Y and inert 2HDM.

The most widely discussed way to avoid FCNC is to introduce a discrete

\mathds{Z}_{2}
symmetry under which the general-basis fields
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
transform
differently.
61
61

61

This also forces
m_{12}^{2}
and
\lambda_{6,7}
to vanish. However,
m_{12}^{2}\neq 0

breaks the discrete symmetry only softly (leading to finite FCNC at one-loop order) and is therefore often included
[
920
]
.

This forces each of the fermion fields to couple to only one
of these scalar doublets, depending on the choice of charge assignment.
Without loss of generality one can assume that the up-type quarks
always couple to
\Phi_{2}
.
There are then four distinct possibilities classified as type I (all fermions couple
to
\Phi_{2}
), type II (
d
and
l
couple to
\Phi_{1}
,
u
to
\Phi_{2}
), type X
(or lepton-specific:
l
couples to
\Phi_{1}
,
u
and
d
to
\Phi_{2}
) and type Y
(
d
couples to
\Phi_{1}
,
u
and
l
to
\Phi_{2}
).
In all these cases, the Yukawa matrices corresponding to the
Higgs basis
Y^{f}_{v}
and
Y^{f}_{\perp}
are then proportional to each
other for each
f=l,d,u
, i.e. one can write

\displaystyle Y^{l}_{\perp}=\zeta_{l}Y^{l}_{v},\qquad Y^{d}_{\perp}=\zeta_{d}Y^{d}_{v},\qquad Y^{u}_{\perp}=\zeta_{u}Y^{u}_{v},

(5.93)

where the values of the proportionality factors
\zeta_{l,d,u}
for the different types depend on
\tan\beta
and
\cot\beta
and are listed in Tab.
5.3
.

A generalisation of the type I, II, X, Y models is given by the
flavour-aligned 2HDM (FA2HDM), proposed and
explored in Refs.
[
933
,
934
]
. In the FA2HDM one
assumes the validity of Eq. (
5.93
) with arbitrary, in
general complex, values of the coefficients
\zeta_{l,d,u}
. Such
general values are not necessarily governed by symmetries, and the
general FA2HDM Yukawa structure is not guaranteed to be stable under
renormalisation. But it provides a very useful
unified framework which contains all types with discrete
symmetries as special cases.

We mention in passing that there is a special case of the scenarios
with discrete symmetries where one of the two Higgs doublets receives
no
vev
, i.e. where
\cot\beta=0
. This is only compatible with
the type I Yukawa couplings in order for all fermions to receive mass.
The resulting model is called the Inert Doublet model. The lightest
BSM scalar is guaranteed to be stable thanks to the unbroken

\mathds{Z}_{2}
symmetry. The Inert Doublet model is therefore of high
interest in view of dark matter. However, since the BSM scalars do not
couple to fermions (unless in generalisations
[
935
,
936
,
937
]
) it provides only tiny contributions to
a_{\mu}
, and

a_{\mu}
does not constrain the Inert Doublet model parameter
space. Hence we will not discuss this case further.

For reference we also provide the Yukawa Lagrangian written in terms
of mass-eigenstate fields. It follows from
Eqs. (
5.92
), (
5.93
) and
(
5.90
)
and reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{L}&\supset-\sum_{f,\mathcal{S}}\Big[Y_{f}^{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{S}\bar{f}\tfrac{M_{f}}{v}\text{P}_{\text{R}}f\Big]+\sqrt{2}H^{+}Y_{l}^{H^{\pm}}\bar{\nu}\tfrac{M_{e}}{v}\text{P}_{\text{R}}e\\
&\hskip 12.80365pt+\sqrt{2}H^{+}\bar{u}\Big[Y^{H^{\pm}}_{d}V_{\text{CKM}}\tfrac{M_{d}}{v}\text{P}_{\text{R}}+Y_{u}^{H^{\pm}}\tfrac{M_{u}}{v}V_{\text{CKM}}\text{P}_{\text{L}}\Big]d+h.c.,\end{split}

(5.94)

where
\mathcal{S}\in\{h,iA,H\}
.
The sum over fermion fields runs over the mass eigenstates
f=e,u
and
d
,
where
M_{e,u,d}
denote the diagonal fermion mass matrices and generation
indices are suppressed. The Yukawa modifier parameters appearing in
the Lagrangian are given by

\displaystyle Y_{f}^{h}=\sin(\beta-\alpha)+\cos(\beta-\alpha)\zeta_{f}

(5.95a)

\displaystyle Y_{f}^{H}=\cos(\beta-\alpha)-\sin(\beta-\alpha)\zeta_{f}

(5.95b)

\displaystyle Y_{d,l}^{A}=Y_{d,l}^{H^{\pm}}=-\zeta_{d,l},\qquad Y_{u}^{A}=Y_{u}^{H^{\pm}}=\zeta_{u},

(5.95c)

such that the couplings to the BSM Higgs states
H,A,H^{\pm}
are
potentially enhanced by the
\zeta_{f}
, where important values
are collected in Tab.
5.3
and the Higgs alignment
limit is given by
\cos(\beta-\alpha)\to 0
.

5.3.2  2HDM contributions to
a_{\mu}

(a)

(b)

Figure 5.14
:
Example of one- and two-loop contributions of the heavy scalars
H,A
and
H^{\pm}
to
a_{\mu}
in the FA2HDM.

In the 2HDM significant corrections to the muon magnetic moment can arise at both the one-loop and two-loop order.
The one-loop diagrams with the exchange of the new Higgs states
H,A,H^{\pm}

shown in Fig.
5.14(a)
are of the generic form given in Eq. (
2.3a
)

\displaystyle\Delta a_{\mu}^{1l}

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\frac{m_{\mu}^{2}}{v^{2}}\bigg\{(Y_{\mu}^{A})^{2}\frac{m_{\mu}^{2}}{M_{A}^{2}}\Big[\ln(\tfrac{m_\mu^2}{M_A^2})+\tfrac{11}{6}\Big]-(Y_{\mu}^{H})^{2}\frac{m_{\mu}^{2}}{M_{H}^{2}}\Big[\ln(\tfrac{m_\mu^2}{M_H^2})+\tfrac{7}{6}\Big]-(Y_{\mu}^{H^{\pm}})^{2}\frac{m_{\mu}^{2}}{6M_{H^{\pm}}^{2}}\bigg\}.

(5.96a)

In the absence of lepton-flavour violating Higgs couplings, these one-loop diagrams are
suppressed by two powers of the muon mass and two additional powers of
the muon Yukawa couplings. Therefore, even if the BSM muon Yukawa coupling is
enhanced by a large value of
\zeta_{l}
, the one-loop diagrams are typically subdominant.
